## Verification log (7 October 2026, author's pass)

**Environment.** Python 3.11.15, NumPy 2.4.6, SciPy 1.17.1, SymPy 1.14.0, mpmath 1.3.0; exact rational arithmetic (`fractions.Fraction`) for every floating-point reference; mpmath at 50 digits where a transcendental reference was needed; float32 runs through `numpy.float32` for every reduced-precision claim. CPython 3.12 with `tools/py/uv.lock` was not available here; CI reruns everything at `docs/PLAN.md` stage 26.

**Recomputed.** Every numeric hook in section 2; both examples of every homework set and every drill's hidden-test references and known-wrong solutions in section 3; both exam blueprints' totals, mixes and worked items in section 4; the exit drills' tolerances; every value in section 7. Every lesson table's quiz column was counted by script.

**Changed while writing.**
- `drl.numerics.barycentric`: the first known-wrong solution (a Vandermonde solve) was accurate on equispaced nodes for $\sin$, so it did not fail; the fifth test now uses 61 Chebyshev nodes, where the Vandermonde solve's maximum error is 1.47 and the barycentric reference's $5.4 \times 10^{-6}$.
- The Romberg drill's ratio test held only from the third level on (ratios 2.91, 2.85, 2.83 for the square-root integrand); the test says so.
- Lesson 61: a first run reused a variable and gave false strike-placement results. Rerun cleanly, a strike on a node and a strike midway between nodes both converge smoothly if the position is kept fixed relative to the grid; only a varying fraction gives erratic ratios (4.2, 1.8, 8.5). The lesson now teaches that.
- Lesson 65: the tree's error at 400 steps is $-0.0050$, not the $-0.0025$ first recalled; the lesson now says the tree needs about 800 steps for $2.5 \times 10^{-3}$ and about 3,300 for $6 \times 10^{-4}$.
- `drl.numerics.rqi`: the first test depended on the random start; it now starts within 0.1 of the eigenvector for 11 with shift 10.9.
- The PD3 drill's known-wrong solution was ill-specified; it now uses the off-grid spot $S_0 = 100.5$ (interpolated error $-0.00016$, nearest node $-0.32$). The MO5 drill's warning-flag test became "$h = 0$ returns exactly 0".
- Exam blueprints recounted: `exm.numerics` format mix (numeric 30 marks, expression 2), applied marks (40 of 80, 50%), coverage (14 of 17 levels); `exm.optim` Q2 maps to level LP2.
- Single and multiple choice were above 40% in courses NL, MO, LP, CX, DA and AP in the first draft; 38 lessons' quiz columns were rewritten. In this pass 10 lessons whose objective begins Show or Derive lacked a W item and gained one (2.0a lessons 12, 30, 35, 46, 54, 85; 2.0b lessons 11, 38, 44, 50).
- Section 1.5's mean-variance objective now matches lesson 48's; decision 19 and request 4 now count 29 modes on 16 widgets (the first draft said 28).
- Reading totals, solving-time shares, the source-id count (37) and the number of papers in the reading map were corrected.

**Counts.** 2.0a: 4 courses, 17 levels, 91 lessons numbered 1 to 91 without gaps. 2.0b: 4 courses, 10 levels, 58 lessons numbered 1 to 58 without gaps. Lesson ids unique; levels of 4 to 7 lessons; level headers agree with their tables. 597 quiz items; choice items 34.5% (2.0a) and 33.6% (2.0b), at most 35.3% in any course. 27 homework sets plus the Numerics Lab (`set.montecarlo.lab`). Hours sum to 120.0 (estimates 113.0) and 80.0 (estimates 75.5). Each exam: 16 items, 80 marks, 32 long-form [counted by script].

**Not checked, tags kept.** Chapter, section, lecture and problem-set numbers marked [check] in sections 2 and 5 (Süli and Mayers chapters 10 and 11, Glasserman's section numbers, Vanderbei's 5th-edition chapters, MIT 18.335J's problem-set topics, searched by canon-gap but not opened here); whether cvxpy-base with Clarabel imports and solves inside Pyodide [unverified], with the fallback of request 5; Pyodide's speed on the larger drills (the 50 by 50 RQI and the Crank-Nicolson pricer at 400 by 400). No claim was found to be invented, so nothing was deleted.

## Verification log (7 October 2026, source and correctness verifier)

**Scope and method.** Every answer in the section 3 examples and drill references, both exam blueprints and their worked items (section 4), and every value in the section 7 lesson was recomputed with Python 3.11.15, NumPy 2.4.6, SciPy 1.17.1, SymPy 1.14.0 and mpmath 1.3.0 (exact rationals or 50-digit references where the point depends on the last digit; my own explicit-scheme, Crank-Nicolson, Higham projection, simplex, KKT and SLSQP runs for the grid, matrix and optimisation answers). Section 2's hooks were outside this pass except where a homework item reuses them.

**Wrong and corrected.**
- Sample lesson, screen 8 (b): `1.0000001 - 1` loses about 7 digits, not 6 (relative error $5.8 \times 10^{-10}$, 9.2 digits left). Screen 9: at $h = 10^{-6}$ the three prices agree to about 7 digits, not 12; the feedback now says the second difference ($1.9 \times 10^{-14}$) is about ten spacings of doubles near 10.45. Every other lesson value reproduced, including the gamma table and the digits-kept row.
- EA1 example A, line 6: near the smallest subnormal the relative error does not approach $\frac12$; it is $\frac13$ at $1.5 \times 2^{-1074}$ and 1 for $x \le 2^{-1075}$.
- EA2 example B: $N = 31$ gives a relative error of $1.5 \times 10^{-16}$ (one ulp), not $9 \times 10^{-16}$.
- EA3 example B: the ratio list was shifted by one; it now starts from $e_0 = 1$.
- EA4 drill: the Vandermonde known-wrong error of 1.47 did not reproduce (1.6 or 0.015 by column order); the true condition number is $4.5 \times 10^{22}$.
- NL1 example B: LAPACK's relative error of 2.5 did not reproduce for any right-hand side tried (0.32 for $A\mathbf 1$, 0.19 to 0.73 over ten random ones).
- MO4 drill: 1,024 Halton points miss $\frac14$ by $1.2 \times 10^{-3}$, so the reference failed its own test; the test now uses 4,096 points ($-3.8 \times 10^{-4}$).
- MO5 drill: the 3.3 standard error holds only for a central difference; the drill now says so.
- LP1 example B: the optimum is not unique (every $w_1 \in [0.5, 0.6]$ gives 0.5%); the answer and grading now say so.
- CX3 example B copied Boyd and Vandenberghe's exercise 5.21, against section 3.0's held-back rule, and cited a wrong section (§5.2.5); it is recreated with new numbers ($p^\star = 3$, $d^\star = 2$), and lesson 31's citation now names the exercise.
- Hours: both budgets sum (120.0 and 80.0; estimates 113.0 and 75.5), but the pace lines contradicted their own block arithmetic: 2.0a is about 11 weeks (Main block 99.75 h binds), 2.0b about 7.5 weeks (67.0 h). The 0.4 spine for MO now includes Glasserman chapter 6, which section 5.4 assigns. The Lab drill id `lab-gauss-hermite` was renamed `lab-gauss-legendre` to match its task (no other file uses it).

**Confirmed.** Every other example answer, rubric total (all 10), set mix and estimate (3 h and 3.5 h), both papers' difficulty, format, applied, generator and course totals, the Q13 and Q3 worked items, the PD3 pricer references ($-0.0025$, ratio 4.0, American put 6.086, off-grid $-0.00016$ against $-0.32$), the CX3, AP1 and AP2 KKT solutions, Almgren and Chriss's sales, and the Monte Carlo variance ratios (0.50, 1.65, 6.9; importance-sampling ratio 585).

**Scope coverage.** Every topic in canon-gap G1 and G2 is taught. Thin spot, not changed: QR factorisation in floating point (Householder against Gram-Schmidt, least squares by QR against the normal equations) appears only in lesson 43's Läuchli example, lesson 48's solver choice and homework NL1; 1.4 holds the first contact. Adam is left to 2.1 lesson 112, as request 3 says.

**Citations.** 16 web searches (one over the cap of 15); WebFetch and direct downloads were blocked by the egress proxy, so no page was opened. Confirmed by search: Süli and Mayers chapters 7, 9, 10 and 11; Wilmott, Howison and Dewynne chapters 7 to 10; Vanderbei 5th ed chapters 4, 5 and 7; Cornuéjols, Peña and Tütüncü 2nd ed chapters 3, 4 and 6; the MIT 18.335J Spring 2019 address and its four problem sets with solutions; the EE364A path; ATAP extended edition (SIAM 2019); Jäckel (2015, *Wilmott* January, doi 10.1002/wilm.10395); Rannacher (1984); Pooley, Vetzal and Forsyth (2003); Caflisch, Morokoff and Owen (1997); O'Neill (2014); Andersen and Broadie (2004); their [check] tags were removed. Still [check]: Higham's section number for cancellation, Boyd and Vandenberghe §3.1.5 and §6.1/§6.3 titles, Glasserman's section numbers, the MIT problem-set numbers per topic, Owen's address, ATAP's chapter numbers, Bottou, Curtis and Nocedal's section numbers, Trefethen and Bau lecture 37. No invented source was found, so nothing was deleted.
